28/// One header-prefixed ATT value produced by ULCP GATT segmentation.
29#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
30pub struct GattSegmentRecord {
31    pub value: Vec<u8>,
32}

34/// A validated property-bearing ULCP frame.
35#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
36pub struct UlcpPropertyFrameRecord {
37    pub transaction_id: u8,
38    pub command: u8,
39    pub property_id: u32,
40    pub value: Vec<u8>,
41}

43/// UI-relevant fields from a validated `PROP_BATTERY` value.
44#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
45pub struct UlcpBatteryRecord {
46    pub percentage: Option<u8>,
47    /// Measured terminal voltage in millivolts, when the device reports it.
48    pub voltage_mv: Option<u16>,
49    /// What the charging system is doing, when the device reports it.
50    /// Whether the radio is on external power follows from this rather
51    /// than being carried separately.
52    pub charge_state: Option<UlcpChargeState>,
53}
54
55/// The charge state a device reports in `PROP_BATTERY`.
56#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Enum)]
57pub enum UlcpChargeState {
58    /// Running off the battery.
59    Discharging,
60    /// On external power, taking charge.
61    Charging,
62    /// On external power, charge complete.
63    Charged,
64}
65
66impl UlcpChargeState {
67    fn from_wire(state: BatteryChargeState) -> Self {
68        match state {
69            BatteryChargeState::Discharging => Self::Discharging,
70            BatteryChargeState::Charging => Self::Charging,
71            BatteryChargeState::Charged => Self::Charged,
72        }
73    }
74}

76/// The device identity's autonomous flood-forwarding policy.
77///
78/// The filter is written as region strings — the same strings the device
79/// advertises as its Supported Regions identity option — while the
80/// default tag is a 2-octet code, because that is what goes on the air
81/// packet by packet. [`region_code_from_string`] and
82/// [`region_code_description`] convert between the two.
83#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
84pub struct UlcpRepeaterSettingsRecord {
85    /// `PROP_MAC_REPEATER_ENABLED`. The remaining fields are inert while
86    /// this is false, but are still read and written.
87    pub enabled: bool,
88    /// `PROP_MAC_REPEATER_REGIONS`: which region-tagged floods to
89    /// forward, as region strings of 1 to 24 octets — a short code, a
90    /// name, or a literal `0x1234`. Empty imposes no regional
91    /// restriction rather than blocking every flood.
92    pub regions: Vec<String>,
93    /// `PROP_MAC_REPEATER_DEFAULT_REGION`: the tag inserted into an
94    /// untagged flood before forwarding it. `None` forwards untagged.
95    pub default_region: Option<Vec<u8>>,
96    /// `PROP_MAC_REPEATER_MIN_RSSI` in dBm. `None` accepts any.
97    pub min_rssi_dbm: Option<i16>,
98    /// `PROP_MAC_REPEATER_MIN_SNR` in whole dB. `None` accepts any.
99    pub min_snr_db: Option<i8>,
100}
101
102/// The device's positioning policy: whether the receiver runs, and what
103/// is done with what it finds.
104///
105/// Read and written as a whole, like [`UlcpRepeaterSettingsRecord`] and
106/// for the same reason — a receiver switched on under half a policy
107/// starts advertising a position nobody just agreed to. `enabled` is
108/// written last so the rest is already in force when it does.
109#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
110pub struct UlcpGnssSettingsRecord {
111    /// `PROP_GNSS_ENABLED`: whether the receiver is powered. Off is the
112    /// lowest power state the board can reach, and on most of them the
113    /// receiver is the largest continuous load there is.
114    pub enabled: bool,
115    /// `PROP_GNSS_IDENT_UPDATE`: whether fixes refresh the location the
116    /// node advertises in its identity.
117    pub ident_update: bool,
118    /// `PROP_GNSS_IDENT_PRECISION`: how many location bytes that
119    /// advertised position is clamped to, 1 (coarsest) through 7. This is
120    /// a disclosure control — see [`ulcp_location_cell_meters`].
121    pub ident_precision: u8,
122    /// `PROP_GNSS_TIME_TRUST`: whether receiver-derived time may set the
123    /// wall clock. Cleared, a hand-set clock is safe from a jammed or
124    /// spoofed sky; position reporting is unaffected.
125    pub time_trust: bool,
126}
127
128/// What the device announces without being asked.
129///
130/// Read and written as a whole, like [`UlcpGnssSettingsRecord`], because
131/// the two schedules are how much of the mesh's airtime this device
132/// claims and an operator sets that as one decision.
133#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
134pub struct UlcpAdvertSettingsRecord {
135    /// `PROP_ADVERT_INTERVAL`: seconds between signed identity
136    /// advertisements, 0 for none. An advertisement reaches only the
137    /// device's own neighbours.
138    pub advert_interval_seconds: u32,
139    /// `PROP_BEACON_INTERVAL`: seconds between empty beacons, 0 for none.
140    /// A beacon floods, collecting the path back to the device as it
141    /// goes, and costs a fraction of an advertisement.
142    pub beacon_interval_seconds: u32,
143    /// `PROP_STARTUP_BEACON`: whether one beacon goes out at bring-up.
144    pub startup_beacon: bool,
145}
146
147/// Where the device says it is: the claim it advertises, not a
148/// measurement.
149///
150/// A position names a cell rather than a point, so the encoded cell is
151/// carried verbatim alongside what it decodes to — the bytes are what a
152/// region proposal needs, because the cell's bounds *are* the
153/// uncertainty, and the degrees are what a readout shows.
154#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, uniffi::Record)]
155pub struct UlcpIdentPositionRecord {
156    /// `PROP_IDENT_LOCATION` verbatim. Empty is a device advertising no
157    /// position, which is a value rather than an absence.
158    pub location: Vec<u8>,
159    /// The center of the advertised cell, absent when there is none.
160    pub latitude_deg: Option<f64>,
161    /// See `latitude_deg`.
162    pub longitude_deg: Option<f64>,
163    /// How wide the advertised cell is at the equator, in meters.
164    pub cell_meters: Option<f64>,
165    /// `PROP_IDENT_ALTITUDE` in whole meters, absent at no stated height.
166    pub altitude_m: Option<i32>,
167}
168
169/// `PROP_GNSS_FIX`: what kind of position solution the receiver has.
170#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Enum)]
171pub enum UlcpFixKind {
172    /// No solution — the receiver is off, or on and still searching.
173    #[default]
174    None,
175    /// Position without altitude.
176    TwoD,
177    /// Position and altitude.
178    ThreeD,
179}
180
181impl UlcpFixKind {
182    fn from_wire(fix: FixKind) -> Self {
183        match fix {
184            FixKind::None => Self::None,
185            FixKind::TwoD => Self::TwoD,
186            FixKind::ThreeD => Self::ThreeD,
187        }
188    }
189}
190
191/// What the receiver currently reports, folded from the five positioning
192/// telemetry properties.
193///
194/// Unlike a battery reading this is carried on *every* snapshot rather
195/// than reported once: it is state the UI mirrors — a map pin does not
196/// disappear because an unrelated property arrived — and the receiver
197/// announces position and fix changes on its own schedule.
198#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, uniffi::Record)]
199pub struct UlcpGnssRecord {
200    pub fix: UlcpFixKind,
201    /// `PROP_GNSS_LOCATION` as it travels: the interleaved
202    /// variable-precision grid code, empty without a fix. Carried
203    /// verbatim so a caller can compare or forward the cell itself
204    /// rather than re-encoding degrees.
205    pub location: Vec<u8>,
206    /// Center of the encoded cell, in degrees. `None` without a fix.
207    ///
208    /// A location names a cell rather than a point; `location_cell_meters`
209    /// says how large that cell is, and rendering a pin without it claims
210    /// a precision the device did not report. Widened from the f32 the
211    /// decoder works in, because that is the shape every consumer of a
212    /// coordinate wants.
213    pub latitude_deg: Option<f64>,
214    pub longitude_deg: Option<f64>,
215    /// Approximate width of the encoded cell at the equator, in meters.
216    pub location_cell_meters: Option<f64>,
217    /// `PROP_GNSS_ALTITUDE` in meters above the WGS-84 ellipsoid.
218    pub altitude_m: Option<i32>,
219    /// `PROP_GNSS_PRECISION`: estimated horizontal accuracy in
220    /// decimeters. An estimate scaled from dilution of precision, not a
221    /// measured error bound.
222    pub accuracy_dm: Option<u16>,
223    /// Satellites contributing to the solution. Reads 0 while the
224    /// receiver is off.
225    pub satellites_used: u8,
226    /// Satellites in view, when the receiver reports them.
227    pub satellites_in_view: Option<u8>,
228}
229
230/// `PROP_TIME`: what the device's wall clock read when it last reported.
231///
232/// Take-once, like a battery reading and for the same reason: a clock
233/// value means nothing without the instant it was received, so a consumer
234/// stamps what arrives. Republishing it on unrelated updates would
235/// restamp a stale reading as a fresh one.
236#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
237pub struct UlcpTimeRecord {
238    /// Seconds since the Unix epoch, or `None` when the device does not
239    /// know what time it is. A device that has never had a fix, a manual
240    /// set, or a retained RTC is in that state, and says so rather than
241    /// reporting zero.
242    pub epoch_seconds: Option<u32>,
243}
244
245/// Read-only, capability-gated device state gathered after host ownership
246/// has been resolved. Counts describe digest forms and contain no key material.
247// Not `Eq`: the advertised position carries decoded degrees, and a
248// coordinate is a measurement rather than an identity. Nothing compares
249// snapshots for anything but equality.
250#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, uniffi::Record)]
251pub struct UlcpSyncRecord {
252    pub capability_count: u32,
253    pub has_host_filtering: bool,
254    pub supports_offline_queue: bool,
255    pub supports_delegated_ack: bool,
256    pub supports_device_name: bool,
257    /// `PROP_DEV_NAME`, when the device has one and reported it. Empty is
258    /// a device with no name rather than a device named "".
259    pub device_name: Option<String>,
260    pub supports_lora: bool,
261    pub supports_duty_cycle_limit: bool,
262    /// The device measures its own power state (`CAP_BATTERY`). A device
263    /// without it never reports a battery, so callers have nothing to show.
264    pub supports_battery: bool,
265    /// `PROP_BATTERY`, when the device measures one and has reported it.
266    ///
267    /// A reading rather than a setting, and absent for a reason that is
268    /// never a fault: on the local link a device announces this on its own
269    /// schedule, so a session that has only just attached has not heard one
270    /// yet. Deliberately not counted among `unreadable_properties` — there
271    /// is no setting here to be written over.
272    pub battery: Option<UlcpBatteryRecord>,
273    /// The device can forward for the mesh on its own (`CAP_REPEATER`).
274    pub supports_repeater: bool,
275    /// The device serves and configures its own advertised node identity
276    /// (`CAP_IDENT`).
277    pub supports_ident: bool,
278    /// The device has an identity domain of its own (`CAP_DEV_IDENTITY`),
279    /// including the `PROP_DEV_PEERS` list.
280    pub supports_device_identity: bool,
281    /// The device keeps a wall clock (`CAP_TIME`). It says nothing about
282    /// where the time comes from, or whether the device currently knows
283    /// it — an unset clock is a device with `CAP_TIME` and no epoch.
284    pub supports_time: bool,
285    /// A GNSS receiver is fitted (`CAP_GNSS`), so the positioning
286    /// properties exist and the device can locate itself.
287    pub supports_gnss: bool,
288    /// The device announces itself on a schedule of its own
289    /// (`CAP_ADVERT`).
290    pub supports_advert: bool,
291    /// The device answers Node Management Requests from the administrators
292    /// it lists (`CAP_ADMIN`). A device without it can only ever be
293    /// configured by whoever is holding it.
294    pub supports_admin: bool,
295    /// The device can make itself conspicuous on request (`CAP_ALERT`).
296    pub supports_alert: bool,
297    /// The device can restart its hardware on request (`CAP_REBOOT`).
298    pub supports_reboot: bool,
299    /// `PROP_ALERT`: what the device is doing to make itself findable, when
300    /// it has said. Live state like `battery`, and absent on the same terms.
301    pub alert: Option<UlcpAlertState>,
302    pub phy_enabled: bool,
303    pub frequency_khz: u32,
304    pub transmit_power_dbm: i8,
305    pub bandwidth_hz: Option<u32>,
306    pub spreading_factor: Option<u8>,
307    pub coding_rate_denom: Option<u8>,
308    pub duty_cycle_now: Option<u16>,
309    pub duty_cycle_limit: Option<u16>,
310    pub saved: Option<SavedSnapshotRecord>,
311    pub queued_frames: Option<u16>,
312    pub dropped_frames: Option<u32>,
313    pub filter_count: Option<u32>,
314    pub host_channel_count: Option<u32>,
315    pub host_peer_count: Option<u32>,
316    pub auto_ack: Option<bool>,
317    /// Present when `supports_repeater` and the device reported the whole
318    /// policy.
319    pub repeater: Option<UlcpRepeaterSettingsRecord>,
320    /// `PROP_DEV_PEERS`: the peer public keys stored on the device
321    /// identity, read back losslessly. Present when
322    /// `supports_device_identity` and the device reported the list.
323    pub dev_peer_keys: Option<Vec<Vec<u8>>>,
324    /// `PROP_DEV_ADMINS`: the node public keys allowed to manage this device
325    /// over the mesh, read back losslessly. Present when `supports_admin`
326    /// and the device reported the list; an empty list is a device that
327    /// nobody may manage remotely.
328    pub dev_admin_keys: Option<Vec<Vec<u8>>>,
329    /// `PROP_DEV_CHANNEL_KEYS`: the two-octet identifiers of the channels the
330    /// device identity has joined. Key material is never read back, so a
331    /// caller names these by deriving identifiers from the keys it holds; one
332    /// that matches nothing locally is a channel the device knows and this
333    /// phone does not.
334    pub dev_channel_ids: Option<Vec<Vec<u8>>>,
335    /// `PROP_IDENT_ROLE`. `None` covers "the device derives its role from
336    /// what it is actually doing", "no `CAP_IDENT`", and "the device would
337    /// not report it" — `supports_ident` and `unreadable_properties`
338    /// distinguish them.
339    pub ident_role: Option<u8>,
340    /// `PROP_IDENT_MOBILE`. Present when `supports_ident` and the device
341    /// reported it.
342    pub ident_mobile: Option<bool>,
343    /// Where the device says it is. Present when `supports_ident` and the
344    /// device reported its position; a device that advertises none reports
345    /// an empty cell rather than nothing.
346    pub ident_position: Option<UlcpIdentPositionRecord>,
347    /// `PROP_DEV_DISCOVERABLE`: whether the device identity answers
348    /// Identity Requests. Present when `supports_device_identity` and the
349    /// device reported it.
350    pub dev_discoverable: Option<bool>,
351    /// `PROP_TZ_OFFSET` in minutes east of UTC. Present when
352    /// `supports_time` and the device reported it.
353    ///
354    /// The zone is configuration and the epoch is not: where a device is
355    /// meant to be is known even when what time it is is not, which is
356    /// why this is here and the clock reading is on the session snapshot.
357    pub tz_offset_min: Option<i16>,
358    /// The positioning policy. Present when `supports_gnss` and the
359    /// device reported the whole of it.
360    pub gnss: Option<UlcpGnssSettingsRecord>,
361    /// The advertisement policy. Present when `supports_advert` and the
362    /// device reported the whole of it.
363    pub advert: Option<UlcpAdvertSettingsRecord>,
364    /// Capability-gated properties the device advertised but would not
365    /// report, in ascending order.
366    ///
367    /// A device that refuses a property — old firmware behind a newer
368    /// capability, a property it never implemented — is a device with an
369    /// unknown setting, not one this phone cannot administer. Their values
370    /// are absent above, they are left out of configuration writes, and
371    /// nothing about them can be verified after a save.
372    pub unreadable_properties: Vec<u32>,
373}
374
375/// `PROP_SAVED`: what the radio reports about its stored snapshot.
376///
377/// `Fallback` and `Unreadable` are the values worth surfacing: the radio
378/// is running on configuration older than the one last saved, or on none
379/// at all, and looks healthy otherwise.
380#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Enum)]
381pub enum SavedSnapshotRecord {
382    /// Nothing is saved.
383    None,
384    /// The newest saved generation is in effect.
385    Current,
386    /// A newer generation was rejected at boot; an older one is in
387    /// effect. Saving again clears it.
388    Fallback,
389    /// A snapshot exists but could not be read; the radio booted with
390    /// factory defaults.
391    Unreadable,
392}

394/// Long-lived host-session phase. Swift maps this value to UI link state but
395/// does not implement ULCP transitions itself.
396#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Enum)]
397pub enum UlcpSessionPhase {
398    Idle,
399    Synchronizing,
400    AwaitingHost,
401    Claiming,
402    Configuring,
403    Attached,
404}

406/// Complete desired live radio configuration. Capability-gated fields must be
407/// omitted when the device does not advertise their associated capability.
408#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
409pub struct UlcpRadioSettingsRecord {
410    pub device_name: Option<String>,
411    pub phy_enabled: bool,
412    pub frequency_khz: u32,
413    pub transmit_power_dbm: i8,
414    pub bandwidth_hz: Option<u32>,
415    pub spreading_factor: Option<u8>,
416    pub coding_rate_denom: Option<u8>,
417    pub duty_cycle_limit: Option<u16>,
418}

420/// Complete desired configuration of a device's *own* domain: what it is
421/// and what it does when no phone is attached.
422///
423/// This is the commissioning counterpart to [`UlcpRadioSettingsRecord`],
424/// which describes only the radio. Every capability-gated field must be
425/// present exactly when the device advertises the matching capability, so
426/// the record always states a whole desired configuration rather than a
427/// patch — a property that a future template feature can lean on.
428#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
429pub struct UlcpDeviceConfigRecord {
430    /// The live radio profile, applied with the same disable-first,
431    /// enable-last ordering [`MobileUlcpSession::configure`] uses.
432    pub radio: UlcpRadioSettingsRecord,
433    /// `PROP_IDENT_ROLE`, or `None` to let the device derive its
434    /// advertised role from what it is actually doing. Requires
435    /// `CAP_IDENT`.
436    pub ident_role: Option<u8>,
437    /// `PROP_IDENT_MOBILE`. Present exactly when the device advertises
438    /// `CAP_IDENT`.
439    pub ident_mobile: Option<bool>,
440    /// `PROP_DEV_DISCOVERABLE`: whether the device identity answers
441    /// Identity Requests. Present exactly when the device advertises
442    /// `CAP_DEV_IDENTITY`.
443    pub dev_discoverable: Option<bool>,
444    /// The flood-forwarding policy. Present exactly when the device
445    /// advertises `CAP_REPEATER`.
446    pub repeater: Option<UlcpRepeaterSettingsRecord>,
447    /// `PROP_TZ_OFFSET` in minutes east of UTC. Present exactly when the
448    /// device advertises `CAP_TIME`.
449    ///
450    /// The clock itself is not here: it is live state rather than
451    /// configuration, is never saved, and is set with
452    /// [`MobileUlcpSession::set_time`].
453    pub tz_offset_min: Option<i16>,
454    /// The positioning policy. Present exactly when the device advertises
455    /// `CAP_GNSS`.
456    pub gnss: Option<UlcpGnssSettingsRecord>,
457    /// The advertisement policy. Present exactly when the device
458    /// advertises `CAP_ADVERT`.
459    pub advert: Option<UlcpAdvertSettingsRecord>,
460}
461
462/// Present one folded [`GnssSnapshot`] as the record Swift sees.
463fn gnss_record(snapshot: &GnssSnapshot) -> UlcpGnssRecord {
464    let bytes = snapshot.location();
465    let placed = (!bytes.is_empty()).then(|| NodeLocation::from_bytes(bytes).center());
466    UlcpGnssRecord {
467        fix: UlcpFixKind::from_wire(snapshot.fix),
468        location: bytes.to_vec(),
469        latitude_deg: placed.map(|(latitude, _)| latitude.into()),
470        longitude_deg: placed.map(|(_, longitude)| longitude.into()),
471        location_cell_meters: (!bytes.is_empty())
472            .then(|| ulcp_location_cell_meters(bytes.len() as u8))
473            .flatten(),
474        altitude_m: snapshot.altitude_m,
475        accuracy_dm: snapshot.accuracy_dm,
476        satellites_used: snapshot.sats_used,
477        satellites_in_view: snapshot.sats_in_view,
478    }
479}
480
481/// Approximate width, at the equator, of the cell one location precision
482/// names — 2,500 km at one byte down to 15 cm at seven. `None` outside
483/// 1–7.
484///
485/// This is what makes a precision mean something to a person: the setting
486/// is a disclosure control, and how much it discloses is an area, not a
487/// byte count. Fractional because the finest two cells are smaller than a
488/// meter, which a whole number could only report as zero.
489#[uniffi::export]
490pub fn ulcp_location_cell_meters(precision_bytes: u8) -> Option<f64> {
491    // 360° of longitude divided into 16^N cells, at 111,320 m per degree.
492    (1..=MAX_PRECISION)
493        .contains(&precision_bytes)
494        .then(|| 360.0 * 111_320.0 / 16f64.powi(precision_bytes.into()))
495}
496
497/// `PROP_ALERT`: what the radio is doing to make itself findable.
498#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Enum)]
499pub enum UlcpAlertState {
500    /// Nothing; the nominal state.
501    None,
502    /// The radio is making itself as conspicuous as its hardware allows
503    /// — beeping, flashing, or both, depending on the board.
504    Locate,
505}
506
507impl UlcpAlertState {
508    fn from_wire(state: AlertState) -> Self {
509        match state {
510            AlertState::None => Self::None,
511            AlertState::Locate => Self::Locate,
512        }
513    }
514
515    fn to_wire(self) -> AlertState {
516        match self {
517            Self::None => AlertState::None,
518            Self::Locate => AlertState::Locate,
519        }
520    }
521}

523/// Authoritative comparison of `PROP_HOST_KEY` with the selected phone identity.
524#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Enum)]
525pub enum UlcpHostOwnership {
526    Unknown,
527    LocalIdentityUnavailable,
528    Unsupported,
529    Unclaimed,
530    Ours,
531    OtherHost,
532}
533
534/// Typed state published after each bounded ULCP-session transition.
535///
536/// Not `Eq`: a position is degrees, and floating point has no total
537/// equality to offer.
538#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, uniffi::Record)]
539pub struct UlcpSessionSnapshotRecord {
540    pub generation: u64,
541    pub phase: UlcpSessionPhase,
542    pub host_ownership: UlcpHostOwnership,
543    pub device_key: Option<Vec<u8>>,
544    pub device_name: Option<String>,
545    pub battery: Option<UlcpBatteryRecord>,
546    /// `PROP_ALERT`, or `None` on a radio without `CAP_ALERT`.
547    ///
548    /// Unlike `battery`, this is carried on *every* snapshot rather than
549    /// reported once: it is state the UI mirrors, and the radio ends an
550    /// alert on its own — a button press or its deadline — so the button
551    /// must follow the radio rather than what the phone last asked for.
552    pub alert: Option<UlcpAlertState>,
553    /// A clock reading that arrived with this update, on a `CAP_TIME`
554    /// device. Reported once — see [`UlcpTimeRecord`].
555    pub time: Option<UlcpTimeRecord>,
556    /// What the receiver reports, on a `CAP_GNSS` device, or `None` until
557    /// the first positioning property is read. Mirrored like `alert`
558    /// rather than taken like `battery`.
559    pub gnss: Option<UlcpGnssRecord>,
560    pub provisioning: Option<UlcpSyncRecord>,
561}

563/// What the platform adapter should do after a completed raw PHY request.
564#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Enum)]
565pub enum UlcpRawTransmitDisposition {
566    Sent,
567    Retry,
568    Rejected,
569}
570
571/// Typed completion of one host-requested raw PHY transmission.
572#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
573pub struct UlcpRawTransmitResultRecord {
574    pub transaction_id: u8,
575    pub status_code: u32,
576    pub status_name: String,
577    pub disposition: UlcpRawTransmitDisposition,
578}
579
580/// A correlated CRP operation completed with a non-OK `PROP_LAST_STATUS`.
581/// This is an operation failure, never evidence that the transport framing is
582/// corrupt or that the BLE connection should be closed.
583#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
584pub struct UlcpOperationErrorRecord {
585    pub operation: String,
586    pub status_code: u32,
587    pub status_name: String,
588}
589
590/// One property value the device announced on its own — `CMD_PROP_IS`
591/// with the unsolicited transaction — as opposed to the answer to
592/// anything this session asked.
593#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
594pub struct UlcpPropertyPushRecord {
595    pub property_id: u32,
596    pub value: Vec<u8>,
597}
598
599/// The completion of one local management operation started with
600/// [`MobileUlcpSession::begin_property_fetch`],
601/// [`begin_property_writes`](MobileUlcpSession::begin_property_writes), or
602/// [`begin_save`](MobileUlcpSession::begin_save).
603///
604/// Answers wear the same record the mesh management path reports, and mean
605/// the same things: a value is what the device holds, a status in its
606/// place is a refusal of that one property. What differs is the carrier —
607/// here the completion rides the session update instead of a mesh event.
608#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
609pub struct UlcpLocalManagementEventRecord {
610    pub answers: Vec<MobileMeshManagementAnswerRecord>,
611    /// The `CMD_SAVE` outcome, on a save. `None` on fetches, on writes,
612    /// and on a save the device has no `CAP_SAVE` to answer.
613    pub status_code: Option<u32>,
614}
615
616/// Work produced by the Rust ULCP session. Frames are complete ULCP
617/// frames; the platform adapter remains responsible for GATT segmentation and
618/// write backpressure.
619#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, uniffi::Record)]
620pub struct UlcpSessionUpdateRecord {
621    pub outbound_frames: Vec<Vec<u8>>,
622    pub received_frames: Vec<UlcpReceivedFrameRecord>,
623    pub snapshot: UlcpSessionSnapshotRecord,
624    pub waiting_for_responses: bool,
625    /// True while one host-requested raw PHY transmission is awaiting the
626    /// radio's `PROP_LAST_STATUS` completion.
627    pub raw_transmit_pending: bool,
628    /// Transaction allocated by `transmit_raw` in this update, if any.
629    pub raw_transmit_started_transaction_id: Option<u8>,
630    /// Completion for the raw PHY transmission consumed by this update.
631    /// Rejections are ordinary radio-level send failures, not malformed
632    /// ULCP frames.
633    pub raw_transmit_result: Option<UlcpRawTransmitResultRecord>,
634    /// Non-transmit operation error consumed by this update. The ULCP
635    /// session has already recovered to a stable stage and remains usable.
636    pub operation_error: Option<UlcpOperationErrorRecord>,
637    /// Completion of the local management operation, when this update
638    /// carries one.
639    pub management_event: Option<UlcpLocalManagementEventRecord>,
640    /// Values the device announced unsolicited with this update, verbatim.
641    /// The snapshot has already absorbed what it recognizes; these carry
642    /// the raw octets to whoever caches values by property number.
643    pub pushed_properties: Vec<UlcpPropertyPushRecord>,
644}
645
646/// One validated raw mesh frame delivered by the companion radio.
647#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Record)]
648pub struct UlcpReceivedFrameRecord {
649    pub data: Vec<u8>,
650    pub rssi_dbm: Option<i16>,
651    pub lqi: Option<u8>,
652    pub snr_cb: Option<i16>,
653    pub was_buffered: bool,
654    pub was_acknowledged: bool,
655    pub age_seconds: u32,
656}
657
658#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
659enum SessionStage {
660    Idle,
661    Initial,
662    Inspection,
663    Refreshing,
664    Claiming,
665    Saving,
666    Configuring,
667    SavingConfiguration,
668    AwaitingHost,
669    Attached,
670}
671
672#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
673enum ExpectedResponse {
674    Property(u32),
675    Claim,
676    Save,
677    /// A `CMD_PROP_SET` of this property. The value written is not kept:
678    /// the device's `CMD_PROP_IS` is what the property is worth, so there
679    /// is nothing to compare it against.
680    ConfigurationProperty(u32),
681    SaveConfiguration,
682    RawTransmit,
683    /// A `CMD_PROP_INSERT` of this key into a device-domain public-key
684    /// table: `PROP_DEV_PEERS` or `PROP_DEV_ADMINS`. The two are the same
685    /// operation on different lists — a table of 32-byte keys the device
686    /// echoes back item by item — so they are confirmed the same way.
687    DevKeyInsert {
688        property: u32,
689        item: Vec<u8>,
690    },
691    /// A `CMD_PROP_REMOVE` of this key from a device-domain public-key table.
692    DevKeyRemove {
693        property: u32,
694        item: Vec<u8>,
695    },
696    /// The `CMD_SAVE` chained behind a device-domain key-table mutation.
697    SaveDevKeys {
698        property: u32,
699    },
700    /// A `CMD_PROP_INSERT` into `PROP_DEV_CHANNEL_KEYS`. Carries the derived
701    /// identifier rather than the key, because the device confirms a channel
702    /// mutation by echoing the identifier — key material is never read back.
703    DevChannelInsert(Vec<u8>),
704    /// A `CMD_PROP_REMOVE` from `PROP_DEV_CHANNEL_KEYS`, selected by key and
705    /// confirmed by identifier.
706    DevChannelRemove(Vec<u8>),
707    /// The `CMD_SAVE` chained behind a device-channel mutation.
708    SaveDevChannels,
709    /// One `CMD_PROP_INSERT` in the host channel-key reconciliation, carrying
710    /// the keys still to be sent. `ALREADY` is success here: a channel key is
711    /// its own item, so a duplicate insert asserts a state that already holds.
712    HostChannelInsert(VecDeque<Vec<u8>>),
713    /// The whole-table `CMD_PROP_SET` used when the device holds a channel
714    /// this phone cannot name, and so cannot select for removal.
715    HostChannelReplace,
716    /// A `CMD_PROP_GET` issued by a local management fetch. Unlike
717    /// `Property`, a refusal is an answer to record, never a stage
718    /// failure: the caller asked an open question about one property.
719    ManagementGet(u32),
720    /// A `CMD_PROP_SET` issued by a local management write, answered by
721    /// the device's echo or a per-property refusal.
722    ManagementSet(u32),
723    /// The `CMD_SAVE` issued by a local management save.
724    ManagementSave,
725}
726
727impl ExpectedResponse {
728    /// Whether this response belongs to the local management operation,
729    /// which is what decides when that operation continues or completes.
730    fn is_management(&self) -> bool {
731        matches!(
732            self,
733            Self::ManagementGet(_) | Self::ManagementSet(_) | Self::ManagementSave
734        )
735    }
736}
737
738/// One local management operation in flight: what is still to ask, what
739/// is still to write, and what the device has answered so far.
740///
741/// The local counterpart of a mesh management exchange, kept to the same
742/// shape deliberately — one operation at a time, answers accumulated
743/// until everything is answered, refusals recorded per property rather
744/// than failing the run.
745#[derive(Debug, Default)]
746struct LocalManagement {
747    fetch_queue: VecDeque<u32>,
748    write_queue: VecDeque<(u32, Vec<u8>)>,
749    answers: Vec<MobileMeshManagementAnswerRecord>,
750    save_status: Option<u32>,
751}
752
753struct UlcpSessionState {
754    generation: u64,
755    /// Which relationship this session represents. Held here, not only on
756    /// the object, because ownership resolution is what it changes: an
757    /// administrative session reports foreign ownership truthfully but
758    /// never waits for a host decision it will not make.
759    mode: UlcpAttachMode,
760    /// Whether post-attach inspection reads only what attaching itself
761    /// requires, leaving everything else to be asked for on demand.
762    lazy_inspection: bool,
763    stage: SessionStage,
764    tids: TidAllocator,
765    expected: HashMap<u8, ExpectedResponse>,
766    selected_host_key: Option<[u8; 32]>,
767    radio_host_key: Option<Vec<u8>>,
768    host_key_unsupported: bool,
769    responses: HashMap<u32, UlcpPropertyFrameRecord>,
770    inspection_queue: VecDeque<u32>,
771    configuration_queue: VecDeque<(u32, Vec<u8>)>,
772    device_key: Option<Vec<u8>>,
773    device_name: Option<String>,
774    /// A battery snapshot this session has received and not yet reported.
775    ///
776    /// Deliberately *not* a cache: it is taken when an update record is
777    /// built, so `UlcpSessionSnapshotRecord::battery` means "a fresh
778    /// measurement arrived with this update" rather than "the last
779    /// measurement ever seen". Battery is live telemetry — a consumer that
780    /// timestamps what it receives would otherwise restamp a minutes-old
781    /// reading on every unrelated update and report it as current.
782    battery: Option<UlcpBatteryRecord>,
783    /// The radio's live `PROP_ALERT`, or `None` until one is read (and
784    /// permanently on a radio without `CAP_ALERT`). Held rather than
785    /// taken: it is a state to mirror, not an event to report once.
786    alert: Option<UlcpAlertState>,
787    /// A `PROP_TIME` reading not yet reported. Taken, for the reason
788    /// [`UlcpTimeRecord`] gives.
789    time: Option<UlcpTimeRecord>,
790    /// The receiver's view of the world, folded from whichever
791    /// positioning properties have arrived. Held: the properties are
792    /// announced separately, so taking it would report a fix without the
793    /// satellite count that came a frame earlier.
794    gnss: Option<GnssSnapshot>,
795    provisioning: Option<UlcpSyncRecord>,
796    stage_failure_pending: bool,
797    /// The local management operation in flight, if any.
798    management: Option<LocalManagement>,
799    /// A completed management operation not yet reported. Taken by the
800    /// next update, like a battery reading.
801    management_event: Option<UlcpLocalManagementEventRecord>,
802    /// Values announced unsolicited and not yet reported, verbatim.
803    pushed_properties: Vec<UlcpPropertyPushRecord>,
804}
805
806impl Default for UlcpSessionState {
807    fn default() -> Self {
808        Self {
809            generation: 0,
810            mode: UlcpAttachMode::Tethered,
811            lazy_inspection: false,
812            stage: SessionStage::Idle,
813            tids: TidAllocator::new(),
814            expected: HashMap::new(),
815            selected_host_key: None,
816            radio_host_key: None,
817            host_key_unsupported: false,
818            responses: HashMap::new(),
819            inspection_queue: VecDeque::new(),
820            configuration_queue: VecDeque::new(),
821            device_key: None,
822            device_name: None,
823            battery: None,
824            alert: None,
825            time: None,
826            gnss: None,
827            provisioning: None,
828            stage_failure_pending: false,
829            management: None,
830            management_event: None,
831            pushed_properties: Vec::new(),
832        }
833    }
834}
835
836/// The two relationships a phone can have with a radio.
837///
838/// They are different things with different lifecycles, and Swift should
839/// model them as different objects: "my radio" is exactly one, tethered,
840/// and re-provisioned on every attach; "radios I administer" is any
841/// number, configured but never claimed. One list must not serve both.
842#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, uniffi::Enum)]
843pub enum UlcpAttachMode {
844    /// This phone is the radio's tethered host: it claims the radio and
845    /// the radio filters, queues and acknowledges on its behalf.
846    #[default]
847    Tethered,
848    /// This phone is administering the radio without claiming it. A
849    /// phone commissioning ten repeaters must not write its host key on
850    /// any of them.
851    Administrative,
852}
853
854/// Stateful mobile host session for the ULCP.
855///
856/// This is the protocol boundary: it consumes complete reassembled ULCP
857/// frames and owns TIDs, response matching, capability-driven synchronization,
858/// host ownership, and claim/save choreography. Platform code owns only the
859/// transport lifecycle, byte shuttling, and timers.
860#[derive(uniffi::Object)]
861pub struct MobileUlcpSession {
862    inner: Mutex<UlcpSessionState>,
863    mode: UlcpAttachMode,
864    lazy_inspection: bool,
865}
866
867#[uniffi::export]
868impl MobileUlcpSession {
869    /// A session for the phone's own radio: the one it tethers to.
870    #[uniffi::constructor]
871    pub fn new() -> Arc<Self> {
872        Arc::new(Self::with_mode(UlcpAttachMode::Tethered))
873    }
874
875    /// A session for a radio this phone administers but does not claim.
876    /// [`Self::claim`] is refused; everything else behaves identically.
877    #[uniffi::constructor]
878    pub fn administrative() -> Arc<Self> {
879        Arc::new(Self::with_mode(UlcpAttachMode::Administrative))
880    }
881
882    /// An administrative session that attaches without reading the device
883    /// whole.
884    ///
885    /// Post-attach inspection is cut to what attaching itself requires —
886    /// the interface check and the always-present radio basics — so the
887    /// link is usable in a couple of exchanges instead of tens. Everything
888    /// else is read on demand through
889    /// [`Self::begin_property_fetch`], which is the point: a settings
890    /// screen that reads lazily has no use for an attach that reads
891    /// everything first.
892    ///
893    /// The provisioning snapshot such a session reports lists every
894    /// unread capability-gated property as unreadable, so the
895    /// whole-record configure calls — which withdraw writes to unreadable
896    /// properties — are not meaningful here. A lazy session writes
897    /// through [`Self::begin_property_writes`].
898    #[uniffi::constructor]
899    pub fn administrative_lazy() -> Arc<Self> {
900        let mut session = Self::with_mode(UlcpAttachMode::Administrative);
901        session.lazy_inspection = true;
902        Arc::new(session)
903    }
904
905    /// Which relationship this session represents.
906    pub fn attach_mode(&self) -> UlcpAttachMode {
907        self.mode
908    }
909
910    /// Begin post-attach synchronization for a new transport generation.
911    pub fn begin(
912        &self,
913        selected_host_key: Option<Vec<u8>>,
914    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
915        let selected_host_key = selected_host_key
916            .map(|key| {
917                key.try_into()
918                    .map_err(|_| MobileError::InvalidPublicKeyLength)
919            })
920            .transpose()?;
921        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
922        let generation = state.generation.wrapping_add(1);
923        *state = UlcpSessionState {
924            generation,
925            mode: self.mode,
926            lazy_inspection: self.lazy_inspection,
927            stage: SessionStage::Initial,
928            selected_host_key,
929            ..UlcpSessionState::default()
930        };
931
932        let mut outbound = Vec::new();
933        for property in [
934            prop::LAST_STATUS,
935            prop::PROTOCOL_VERSION,
936            prop::CAPS,
937            prop::DEV_KEY,
938            prop::DEV_NAME,
939            prop::BATTERY,
940            prop::HOST_KEY,
941        ] {
942            outbound.push(state.get_property(property)?);
943        }
944        Ok(state.update(outbound))
945    }
946
947    /// Replace an unclaimed or other-host configuration with this phone's key.
948    pub fn claim(&self, host_key: Vec<u8>) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
949        // Commissioning is not tethering: an administrative session
950        // configures the radio's own domain and never writes a host key.
951        if self.mode == UlcpAttachMode::Administrative {
952            return Err(MobileError::AdministrativeSession);
953        }
954        let host_key: [u8; 32] = host_key
955            .try_into()
956            .map_err(|_| MobileError::InvalidPublicKeyLength)?;
957        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
958        if state.stage != SessionStage::AwaitingHost
959            || !matches!(
960                state.ownership(),
961                UlcpHostOwnership::Unclaimed | UlcpHostOwnership::OtherHost
962            )
963        {
964            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
965        }
966        state.selected_host_key = Some(host_key);
967        state.stage = SessionStage::Claiming;
968        state.expected.clear();
969        let tid = state.allocate_tid();
970        state.expected.insert(tid, ExpectedResponse::Claim);
971        let frame = ulcp_prop_set(tid, prop::HOST_KEY, host_key.to_vec())?;
972        Ok(state.update(vec![frame]))
973    }
974
975    /// Erase ALL mutable state on the radio (saved provisioning, device
976    /// identity, BLE bonds, pairing PIN, every persisted journal) and
977    /// reboot it. The radio does not reply — the reset drops the link —
978    /// so this is fire-and-forget: send the frame, then treat the ensuing
979    /// disconnect as completion. Permitted from any stage so a misbehaving
980    /// radio can always be wiped; unlike `claim`/`configure` it makes no
981    /// stage or ownership demands.
982    pub fn factory_reset(&self) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
983        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
984        let tid = state.allocate_tid();
985        // Deliberately no ExpectedResponse: the device wipes storage and
986        // reboots without answering, so `update` reports
987        // waiting_for_responses = false and the caller does not block.
988        let frame = ulcp_factory_reset(tid)?;
989        Ok(state.update(vec![frame]))
990    }
991
992    /// Restart the radio (`CMD_REBOOT`), keeping everything it has
993    /// persisted. Fire-and-forget for the same reason
994    /// [`Self::factory_reset`] is: a radio that restarts answers nothing
995    /// and the reboot drops the link. A radio without `CAP_REBOOT`
996    /// answers `STATUS_UNIMPLEMENTED` instead, which arrives as an
997    /// ordinary unsolicited status.
998    ///
999    /// Permitted from any stage, again like the factory reset: a radio
1000    /// worth restarting is often one that is not answering properly.
1001    pub fn reboot(&self) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1002        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1003        let tid = state.allocate_tid();
1004        let frame = ulcp_reboot(tid)?;
1005        Ok(state.update(vec![frame]))
1006    }
1007
1008    /// Start or stop the radio's locate alert (`PROP_ALERT`) so a
1009    /// misplaced radio can be found.
1010    ///
1011    /// Not part of `configure_device`, and never saved: this is live
1012    /// behavior rather than configuration, and it deliberately survives
1013    /// the phone walking out of BLE range — which is precisely when a
1014    /// search needs it. What ends it is this call, a button press at the
1015    /// radio, or the radio's own deadline; the latter two arrive as an
1016    /// unsolicited `PROP_ALERT` carried on the session snapshot.
1017    ///
1018    /// Re-sending `Locate` while an alert is running restarts that
1019    /// deadline, which is how a longer search keeps the alert alive.
1020    pub fn set_alert(&self, state: UlcpAlertState) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1021        let mut session = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1022        if session.stage != SessionStage::Attached {
1023            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
1024        }
1025        if !session.has_capability(cap::ALERT)? {
1026            return Err(MobileError::UnsupportedCapability);
1027        }
1028        let value = encode_alert_state(state)?;
1029        let tid = session.allocate_tid();
1030        session
1031            .expected
1032            .insert(tid, ExpectedResponse::Property(prop::ALERT));
1033        let frame = ulcp_prop_set(tid, prop::ALERT, value)?;
1034        Ok(session.update(vec![frame]))
1035    }
1036
1037    /// Set — or clear — the device's wall clock (`PROP_TIME`).
1038    ///
1039    /// Live state rather than configuration, and never saved: an epoch
1040    /// written to flash would come back arbitrarily wrong, since nothing
1041    /// bounds how long a device spends powered off. So this is not part
1042    /// of [`Self::configure_device`], which carries the time *zone* —
1043    /// where the device is meant to be is worth persisting even when what
1044    /// time it is is not.
1045    ///
1046    /// `None` clears the clock back to unknown, which is what a device
1047    /// reports before its first fix. On a device whose receiver is
1048    /// trusted for time, a fix will overwrite whatever is set here.
1049    ///
1050    /// The device answers with the epoch it now holds; that answer, not
1051    /// the value written, is what the session snapshot reports.
1052    pub fn set_time(
1053        &self,
1054        epoch_seconds: Option<u32>,
1055    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1056        let mut session = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1057        if session.stage != SessionStage::Attached {
1058            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
1059        }
1060        if !session.has_capability(cap::TIME)? {
1061            return Err(MobileError::UnsupportedCapability);
1062        }
1063        let value = epoch_seconds
1064            .map(|epoch| epoch.to_le_bytes().to_vec())
1065            .unwrap_or_default();
1066        let tid = session.allocate_tid();
1067        session
1068            .expected
1069            .insert(tid, ExpectedResponse::Property(prop::TIME));
1070        let frame = ulcp_prop_set(tid, prop::TIME, value)?;
1071        Ok(session.update(vec![frame]))
1072    }
1073
1074    /// Apply, verify, and persist a complete radio-settings snapshot.
1075    pub fn configure(
1076        &self,
1077        settings: UlcpRadioSettingsRecord,
1078    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1079        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1080        if state.stage != SessionStage::Attached {
1081            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
1082        }
1083        validate_radio_settings(&settings, DeviceCapabilities::read(&state)?)?;
1084
1085        state.expected.clear();
1086        state.configuration_queue = state.writable(configuration_values(settings, Vec::new()));
1087        let mut outbound = Vec::new();
1088        state.start_configuration(&mut outbound)?;
1089        Ok(state.update(outbound))
1090    }
1091
1092    /// Apply, verify, and persist a complete configuration of the device's
1093    /// own domain: its radio, the role it advertises, and whether and how
1094    /// it forwards for the mesh on its own.
1095    ///
1096    /// This is what commissioning writes. It touches nothing in the host
1097    /// domain — no host key, no filters, no queues — so it is equally
1098    /// valid from an administrative session on someone else's radio and
1099    /// from a tethered session on this phone's own.
1100    pub fn configure_device(
1101        &self,
1102        configuration: UlcpDeviceConfigRecord,
1103    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1104        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1105        if state.stage != SessionStage::Attached {
1106            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
1107        }
1108        let capabilities = DeviceCapabilities::read(&state)?;
1109        validate_radio_settings(&configuration.radio, capabilities)?;
1110        let device_values = validate_device_settings(&configuration, capabilities)?;
1111
1112        state.expected.clear();
1113        state.configuration_queue =
1114            state.writable(configuration_values(configuration.radio, device_values));
1115        let mut outbound = Vec::new();
1116        state.start_configuration(&mut outbound)?;
1117        Ok(state.update(outbound))
1118    }
1119
1120    /// Apply and persist the time zone and the positioning policy, and
1121    /// nothing else.
1122    ///
1123    /// [`Self::configure_device`] can write these too, as part of a whole
1124    /// device domain — that is what commissioning does. This exists for
1125    /// the case commissioning does not cover: a phone changing the
1126    /// positioning settings of the radio it is *tethered* to, which has
1127    /// no reason to restate that radio's role, discoverability, or
1128    /// forwarding policy in order to switch a receiver on.
1129    ///
1130    /// Each argument must be present exactly when the device advertises
1131    /// the matching capability, and the four positioning properties
1132    /// travel together for the reason [`UlcpGnssSettingsRecord`] gives.
1133    /// The write is echo-verified property by property and closed with a
1134    /// save, like any other configuration pass.
1135    pub fn configure_positioning(
1136        &self,
1137        gnss: Option<UlcpGnssSettingsRecord>,
1138        tz_offset_min: Option<i16>,
1139    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1140        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1141        if state.stage != SessionStage::Attached {
1142            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
1143        }
1144        let values = positioning_values(gnss, tz_offset_min, DeviceCapabilities::read(&state)?)?;
1145        // A radio with neither capability has nothing here to configure,
1146        // which is a caller mistake rather than an empty success.
1147        if values.is_empty() {
1148            return Err(MobileError::UnsupportedCapability);
1149        }
1150
1151        state.expected.clear();
1152        state.configuration_queue = state.writable(values);
1153        let mut outbound = Vec::new();
1154        state.start_configuration(&mut outbound)?;
1155        Ok(state.update(outbound))
1156    }
1157
1158    /// Apply and persist the advertisement policy, and nothing else.
1159    ///
1160    /// The tethered-radio counterpart of [`Self::configure_positioning`]:
1161    /// a phone changing how often its own radio announces itself has no
1162    /// reason to restate that radio's role, forwarding policy, or
1163    /// receiver settings to do it.
1164    pub fn configure_advertising(
1165        &self,
1166        advert: Option<UlcpAdvertSettingsRecord>,
1167    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1168        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1169        if state.stage != SessionStage::Attached {
1170            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
1171        }
1172        let values = advert_values(advert, DeviceCapabilities::read(&state)?)?;
1173        // A radio that announces nothing on its own has nothing here to
1174        // configure, which is a caller mistake rather than a no-op.
1175        if values.is_empty() {
1176            return Err(MobileError::UnsupportedCapability);
1177        }
1178
1179        state.expected.clear();
1180        state.configuration_queue = state.writable(values);
1181        let mut outbound = Vec::new();
1182        state.start_configuration(&mut outbound)?;
1183        Ok(state.update(outbound))
1184    }
1185
1186    /// Re-read every capability-gated property represented by the mobile
1187    /// snapshot. The existing snapshot remains usable while the bounded
1188    /// refresh is in flight; authoritative provisioning is published when
1189    /// the full capability-gated read completes.
1190    pub fn refresh(&self) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1191        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1192        if state.stage != SessionStage::Attached || !state.expected.is_empty() {
1193            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
1194        }
1195        let capabilities = state
1196            .responses
1197            .get(&prop::CAPS)
1198            .ok_or(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame)?
1199            .value
1200            .clone();
1201        state.inspection_queue = ulcp_refresh_properties(capabilities)?.into();
1202        let mut outbound = Vec::new();
1203        state.start_refresh(&mut outbound)?;
1204        Ok(state.update(outbound))
1205    }
1206
1207    /// Sample where the device is, and how well it knows.
1208    ///
1209    /// The device announces a fix indicator and nothing else about a
1210    /// position — a receiver reports about a fix a second and ordinary
1211    /// noise moves the reading, so announcing any of this would keep the
1212    /// radio transmitting for a host that may not be looking. A host that
1213    /// *is* looking asks, at whatever rate it can use the answer.
1214    ///
1215    /// Deliberately narrower than [`refresh`](Self::refresh): the five
1216    /// positioning properties and nothing else, so a screen watching a
1217    /// position does not re-read the PHY triple every time it looks.
1218    pub fn refresh_positioning(&self) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1219        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1220        if state.stage != SessionStage::Attached || !state.expected.is_empty() {
1221            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
1222        }
1223        let capabilities = state
1224            .responses
1225            .get(&prop::CAPS)
1226            .ok_or(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame)?
1227            .value
1228            .clone();
1229        // A device without the capability has nothing to sample, and
1230        // asking anyway would earn a refusal per property.
1231        if !decode_capabilities(&capabilities)?.contains(&cap::GNSS) {
1232            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
1233        }
1234        state.inspection_queue = VecDeque::from(vec![
1235            prop::GNSS_LOCATION,
1236            prop::GNSS_ALTITUDE,
1237            prop::GNSS_FIX,
1238            prop::GNSS_PRECISION,
1239            prop::GNSS_SATELLITES,
1240        ]);
1241        let mut outbound = Vec::new();
1242        state.start_refresh(&mut outbound)?;
1243        Ok(state.update(outbound))
1244    }
1245
1246    /// Read the named properties, whatever they are, and answer with what
1247    /// the device said about each.
1248    ///
1249    /// The local counterpart of a mesh management fetch, and it reports
1250    /// the same way: one answer per property, a refusal recorded as that
1251    /// property's status rather than failing the run. The completion
1252    /// arrives as [`UlcpSessionUpdateRecord::management_event`] once every
1253    /// answer is in. One operation may run at a time.
1254    ///
1255    /// Values read fold into the session's own snapshot as well, so a
1256    /// settings screen reading a property does not leave the attached
1257    /// provisioning stale.
1258    pub fn begin_property_fetch(
1259        &self,
1260        property_ids: Vec<u32>,
1261    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1262        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1263        state.begin_local_management()?;
1264        state.management = Some(LocalManagement {
1265            fetch_queue: property_ids.into(),
1266            ..LocalManagement::default()
1267        });
1268        let mut outbound = Vec::new();
1269        state.continue_local_management(&mut outbound)?;
1270        Ok(state.update(outbound))
1271    }
1272
1273    /// Write the given properties, in the given order, and answer with
1274    /// what the device says each is now worth.
1275    ///
1276    /// The order is the caller's to state and is preserved — a dirty-write
1277    /// plan brackets the radio with `PROP_PHY_ENABLED`, and reordering it
1278    /// would ask the device to retune mid-transmission. Writes go out one
1279    /// at a time for the same reason. A refusal is recorded as that
1280    /// property's answer and the run continues, matching the mesh path.
1281    ///
1282    /// Nothing is saved: persistence is a separate, explicit
1283    /// [`Self::begin_save`], again matching the mesh path.
1284    pub fn begin_property_writes(
1285        &self,
1286        writes: Vec<MobileMeshPropertyWriteRecord>,
1287    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1288        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1289        state.begin_local_management()?;
1290        state.management = Some(LocalManagement {
1291            write_queue: writes
1292                .into_iter()
1293                .map(|write| (write.property_id, write.value))
1294                .collect(),
1295            ..LocalManagement::default()
1296        });
1297        let mut outbound = Vec::new();
1298        state.continue_local_management(&mut outbound)?;
1299        Ok(state.update(outbound))
1300    }
1301
1302    /// Persist whatever the device is holding, reporting the `CMD_SAVE`
1303    /// status on the completion event.
1304    ///
1305    /// On a device without `CAP_SAVE` there is nothing to ask, and the
1306    /// operation completes immediately with no status — running
1307    /// configuration is all such a device has.
1308    pub fn begin_save(&self) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1309        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1310        state.begin_local_management()?;
1311        if !state.has_capability(cap::SAVE)? {
1312            state.management_event = Some(UlcpLocalManagementEventRecord {
1313                answers: Vec::new(),
1314                status_code: None,
1315            });
1316            return Ok(state.update(Vec::new()));
1317        }
1318        state.management = Some(LocalManagement::default());
1319        let tid = state.allocate_tid();
1320        state.expected.insert(tid, ExpectedResponse::ManagementSave);
1321        let frame = ulcp_save(tid)?;
1322        Ok(state.update(vec![frame]))
1323    }
1324
1325    /// Store one channel key on the radio's device identity
1326    /// (`PROP_DEV_CHANNEL_KEYS`), then persist with a chained `CMD_SAVE` when
1327    /// the device can.
1328    ///
1329    /// This is the device's own channel membership, independent of the phone's:
1330    /// it is what the device uses for its own advertisements, blind-unicast
1331    /// addressing, and repeater filtering, and it survives host replacement.
1332    ///
1333    /// Requires an attached, otherwise-idle session on a device advertising
1334    /// `CAP_DEV_IDENTITY`, and the device additionally requires an encrypted
1335    /// link before it will accept key material. Failures surface as
1336    /// `operation_error` with the device's status name — `NOMEM` when the list
1337    /// is full (capacity [`ulcp_max_dev_channels`]), `ALREADY` when the key is
1338    /// already stored, which callers should treat as success.
1339    pub fn insert_device_channel_key(
1340        &self,
1341        channel_key: Vec<u8>,
1342    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1343        let id = dev_channel_id(&channel_key)?;
1344        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1345        state.begin_device_domain_operation(cap::DEV_IDENTITY)?;
1346        let tid = state.allocate_tid();
1347        state
1348            .expected
1349            .insert(tid, ExpectedResponse::DevChannelInsert(id));
1350        let frame = ulcp_prop_insert(tid, prop::DEV_CHANNEL_KEYS, &channel_key)?;
1351        Ok(state.update(vec![frame]))
1352    }
1353
1354    /// Remove one channel key from the radio's device identity
1355    /// (`PROP_DEV_CHANNEL_KEYS`), then persist with a chained `CMD_SAVE` when
1356    /// the device can.
1357    ///
1358    /// The remove selector is the key itself, so only a channel the caller
1359    /// still holds the key for can be removed this way. Same preconditions as
1360    /// [`Self::insert_device_channel_key`]; `ITEM_NOT_FOUND` surfaces as
1361    /// `operation_error` and callers should treat it as success.
1362    pub fn remove_device_channel_key(
1363        &self,
1364        channel_key: Vec<u8>,
1365    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1366        let id = dev_channel_id(&channel_key)?;
1367        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1368        state.begin_device_domain_operation(cap::DEV_IDENTITY)?;
1369        let tid = state.allocate_tid();
1370        state
1371            .expected
1372            .insert(tid, ExpectedResponse::DevChannelRemove(id));
1373        let frame = ulcp_prop_remove(tid, prop::DEV_CHANNEL_KEYS, &channel_key)?;
1374        Ok(state.update(vec![frame]))
1375    }
1376
1377    /// Make the radio's host channel-key table (`PROP_HOST_CHANNEL_KEYS`)
1378    /// match the phone identity's joined channels.
1379    ///
1380    /// The radio needs these keys to recognize multicast and blind-unicast
1381    /// traffic addressed to channels this phone has joined, and to queue it
1382    /// while the phone is away. That is bookkeeping between the app and its
1383    /// own radio, not a user-facing setting: callers reconcile on attach and
1384    /// after every join or leave, and never surface it.
1385    ///
1386    /// The host domain is volatile — the device does not persist it — so no
1387    /// `CMD_SAVE` is chained and reconciling on attach is what makes it stick.
1388    /// Requires an attached, idle session on a device advertising
1389    /// `CAP_HOST_KEYS`; otherwise the table is not this session's to manage
1390    /// and the call reports that the capability is missing.
1391    ///
1392    /// Returns without any frames when the device already holds exactly the
1393    /// requested set.
1394    pub fn reconcile_host_channel_keys(
1395        &self,
1396        keys: Vec<Vec<u8>>,
1397    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1398        let mut desired = VecDeque::with_capacity(keys.len());
1399        let mut desired_ids = Vec::with_capacity(keys.len());
1400        for key in keys {
1401            desired_ids.push(dev_channel_id(&key)?);
1402            desired.push_back(key);
1403        }
1404
1405        let mut state = self.inner.lock().expect("ULCP session mutex poisoned");
1406        if state.stage != SessionStage::Attached || !state.expected.is_empty() {
1407            return Err(MobileError::InvalidUlcpFrame);
1408        }
1409        if !state.has_capability(cap::HOST_KEYS)? {
1410            return Err(MobileError::UnsupportedCapability);
1411        }
1412
1413        let current = state
1414            .responses
1415            .get(&prop::HOST_CHANNEL_KEYS)
1416            .map(|entry| entry.value.clone())
1417            .unwrap_or_default();
1418        let current_ids: Vec<Vec<u8>> = current
1419            .chunks(items::CHANNEL_ID_LEN)
1420            .map(<[u8]>::to_vec)
1421            .collect();
1422
1423        // Shedding a channel needs its key as the remove selector, and an
1424        // identifier this phone cannot derive is one whose key it does not
1425        // hold. The table is small, so one whole-table write says everything.
1426        if current_ids.iter().any(|id| !desired_ids.contains(id)) {
1427            let table: Vec<u8> = desired.iter().flatten().copied().collect();
1428            let tid = state.allocate_tid();
1429            state
1430                .expected
1431                .insert(tid, ExpectedResponse::HostChannelReplace);
1432            state.set_host_channel_ids(&desired_ids);
1433            let frame = ulcp_prop_set(tid, prop::HOST_CHANNEL_KEYS, table)?;
1434            return Ok(state.update(vec![frame]));
1435        }
1436
1437        desired.retain(|key| {
1438            !current_ids
1439                .iter()
1440                .any(|id| dev_channel_id(key).is_ok_and(|derived| &derived == id))
1441        });
1442        match state.next_host_channel_insert(desired) {
1443            Some(frame) => Ok(state.update(vec![frame])),
1444            None => Ok(state.update(Vec::new())),
1445        }
1446    }
1447
1448    /// Store one peer public key on the radio's device identity
1449    /// (`PROP_DEV_PEERS`), then persist with a chained `CMD_SAVE` when the
1450    /// device can.
1451    ///
1452    /// Requires an attached, otherwise-idle session on a device advertising
1453    /// `CAP_DEV_IDENTITY`. Failures surface as `operation_error` with the
1454    /// device's status name — `NOMEM` when the list is full (capacity
1455    /// [`ulcp_max_dev_peers`]), `ALREADY` when the key is already stored,
1456    /// which callers should treat as success.
1457    pub fn insert_device_peer(
1458        &self,
1459        public_key: Vec<u8>,
1460    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1461        self.insert_device_key(cap::DEV_IDENTITY, prop::DEV_PEERS, public_key)
1462    }

1464    /// Remove one peer public key from the radio's device identity
1465    /// (`PROP_DEV_PEERS`), then persist with a chained `CMD_SAVE` when the
1466    /// device can.
1467    ///
1468    /// Same preconditions as [`Self::insert_device_peer`]. `ITEM_NOT_FOUND`
1469    /// surfaces as `operation_error` and callers should treat it as success —
1470    /// the key is not on the device either way.
1471    pub fn remove_device_peer(
1472        &self,
1473        public_key: Vec<u8>,
1474    ) -> Result<UlcpSessionUpdateRecord, MobileError> {
1475        self.remove_device_key(cap::DEV_IDENTITY, prop::DEV_PEERS, public_key)
1476    }
